   https://www.acc.com/sites/default/files/2026-01/2026-ACC-Chief-Legal-Officers-Survey-Key-Findings.pdf
23. **LegalOps.com's founders frame the GC's evolution as four paradigms (risk manager, business partner, strategic and trusted adviser, competitive advantage), each producing a distinct legal operations role from tactical cost-cutting to driving strategy.** [library-only, writer waived fact-check; confidence high; trade-press; 2023-07-24; usable by 2026-06-14]
   ABA Journal, Ari Kaplan interview with Connie Brenton and Jeff Franke. Verdict: confirmed (fact-checker read the page; quotes verbatim). Franke's full sentence on the roles contains an em dash in the original: "Those four paradigms result in four distinct legal operations roles [em dash] from tactical, cost-cutting and efficiency-focused to supporting and driving strategy." Source is from 2023. Driver 3.
   Quote: "the ultimate paradigm is the general counsel who is helping to create a competitive advantage for the business"
   https://www.abajournal.com/columns/article/redefining-legal-operations
24. **The line 'Legal operations is no longer just a support function, it is a platform for strategic leadership' is CCBJ's editorial standfirst, not a Connie Brenton quote; Franke's confirmed line is that no blueprint for legal operations exists yet and LegalOps.com is building one.** [library-only, writer waived fact-check; confidence high; trade-press; 2025-08-06; usable by 2026-06-14]
   Verdict: corrected (attribution). Both recorded quotes contain em dashes in the original, so paraphrase under house style. Franke verbatim: "There is no blueprint for Legal Operations [em dash] yet. But we are building one. And we are doing it together with the community." Driver 3.
   https://ccbjournal.com/articles/legal-ops-redefined-connie-brenton-jeff-franke-on-data-development-and-strategic-leadership
25. **47% of GCs say they are more focused on service enhancement than cost reduction, and only 7% prioritize cost reduction; legal ops professionals split 36% service versus 22% cost.** [library-only, writer waived fact-check; confidence high; primary; 2025-09-24; usable by 2026-06-14]
   TR 2025 LDO Index, p.7; verdict confirmed. Caution: a different 47% of GCs focus equally on both; do not conflate. Supports Driver 3 (GC orientation toward the business) and Driver 1.
   https://www.thomsonreuters.com/en-us/posts/wp-content/uploads/sites/20/2025/09/Legal-Department-Operations-Index-2025.pdf

Gaps:
- Three writer-named figures are outside the 2026-06-14 window and cannot be cited in this article: Deloitte UK 84% not-redesigned-roles and 72%-to-44% hourly work (2026-07-09, usable from 2026-07-10), Axiom 7% scaled and 83% cannot measure (2026-06-29 / 2026-07-09, usable from 2026-07-10), Gartner 56% headcount freeze (Lawyers Weekly 2026-07-28, usable from 2026-09-01). In-window substitutes: Harbor 2026-05-11, ACC 63%, Axiom 2026-02-17 GC survey, Axiom 2025-09-08 budgeting survey.
- Deloitte US '2026 Predictions for Chief Legal Officers' has a 2026 copyright but no publication date in the library or on the document; treat as very likely in-window but confirm the date or cite with an access date.
- KPMG 2026 Global General Counsel Outlook: no publication month recorded and none of its figures or quotes were fact-checked (KPMG was unreachable). Fieldwork ended February 2026. Needs a date check and a verbatim check before use.
- No board-level or CEO-level survey on legal or risk exists in the library. Closest items: TR's C-suite comparison (17%; sample size not stated), ACC's CLO-reported board attendance (79%), Axiom's CFO-inclusive budgeting survey (18-point authority gap). A CEO or board survey would need new research.
- Gartner on legal departments and business risk: the library holds only the 2025-10-01 GC priorities survey (104 GCs; 36% AI-focused, 9% contract analytics) via a secondary reprint, and a 2025-12-17 release known by title only. Neither states that legal or risk is recognised as critical to the business.
- EY 2025 budget and operating-model figures (83%, 87%, 11%, 20%, 60% ALSP) rest on search snippets and were never fetched; only the data-quality set (52%, 44%, 41%, 75%) was writer-verified in May 2026.
- ACC/MLA 2026 Benchmarking Report (legal spend 0.43% of revenue, six-year low) is dated only by its file path (2026-06) and its first dated coverage is 2026-07-09, so it cannot be safely placed before 2026-06-14; its copyright also requires 'Reprinted with permission' wording on extracts.
- Quotes recorded via the summarizing fetch tool and never seen verbatim by a fact-checker: Kevin Clem 'paradox' (Lexpert), Jason L. Brown (Lexpert), both KPMG quotes, Snell 2026-03-10 blog wording, Gio DiLuca (ACC Corporate Counsel Now), and the phrase 'the enterprise's central authority for non-financial risk' attributed to ACC. Do not print inside quotation marks without a verbatim check.
- The Substack figure that 62% of CLOs are regularly sought for strategic input (up from 43%) is unverified against the gated full ACC report; do not print.
- Several verbatim quotes for Driver 3 (ABA Journal Franke, CCBJ Franke and standfirst) contain em dashes in the original and must be paraphrased or restructured under house style.


## research:r2-km-ai-alignment

Round-2 research for the knowledge management article, written to the notes file with a Part A mapping table. Part A: every requested AI term now has a definition and at least one dated source that passes the 2026-09-14 display date (Anthropic engineering post 2025-09-29 for context, context engineering, system prompts, tools, few-shot; Willison 2025-06-27 for the Lütke and Karpathy origin quotes; Lewis 2020 for RAG; Brown 2020 for few-shot; Anthropic Agent Skills 2025-10-16; Microsoft Learn pages dated April to August 2026 for embeddings, chunking, grounding data; Vals 2025-02-27 and the Stanford RegLab 2025 journal version for evaluation; OpenAI's Harvey case study 2024-04-02 and LexisNexis 2024-05-03 for fine-tuning versus retrieval). Undated vendor docs are flagged. Part B: OWASP LLM08:2025 (non-vendor) and the UT Austin ConfusedPilot preprint state the permission-leak risk; Microsoft Learn's semantic index, security, and Graph connector ACL pages document permission trimming, all dated before the display date. Part C: all five gap-track items re-read as raw text; wording, dates, and attributions confirmed, with two refinements (Harbor's own "0%" sentence includes in-house departments; the CCBJ "record quality" line is Calve's, not Walker's). CLOC agenda entry pulled from the live Sessionboard feed with time, room, tags, and speakers. Part D: no in-house KM headcount benchmark exists, but the CLOC 2025 State of the Industry PDF (186 departments) shows 79% place knowledge management within legal operations responsibilities, up from 71% per Above the Law, with a median legal ops function of 5 FTEs. This is the survey evidence the idea file listed as missing.

1. **Anthropic defines context as the set of tokens included when sampling from an LLM and context engineering as strategies for curating and maintaining the optimal set of tokens during inference.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; primary; 2025-09-29; usable by 2026-06-14]
   Engineering blog post by Prithvi Rajasekaran, Ethan Dixon, Carly Ryan, and Jeremy Hadfield. Also introduces context rot and the attention budget, says few-shot examples are a well known best practice, and defines tools as a way agents pull in additional context. Read through the fetch tool; wording consistent across two summaries.
   Quote: "Context engineering refers to the set of strategies for curating and maintaining the optimal set of tokens (information) during LLM inference"
   https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/effective-context-engineering-for-ai-agents
2. **The term context engineering spread in June 2025 through posts by Tobi Lütke and Andrej Karpathy, both reproduced on Simon Willison's blog.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; other; 2025-06-27; usable by 2026-06-14]
   Lütke: "the art of providing all the context for the task to be plausibly solvable by the LLM." Karpathy: "the delicate art and science of filling the context window with just the right information for the next step." The X posts themselves were not fetched; Willison's page is the reachable copy.
   Quote: "the delicate art and science of filling the context window with just the right information for the next step"
   https://simonwillison.net/2025/Jun/27/context-engineering/
3. **Anthropic's Agent Skills are folders of instructions, scripts, and resources loaded only when relevant, presented as a way to package organizational expertise.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; primary; 2025-10-16; usable by 2026-06-14]
   Announcement post. The open standard at agentskills.io (undated) adds that skills package "procedural knowledge and company-, team-, and user-specific context into portable, version-controlled folders" and names legal review processes as an example.
   Quote: "Skills are folders that include instructions, scripts, and resources that Claude can load when needed."
   https://claude.com/blog/skills
4. **Retrieval-augmented generation originates in Lewis et al. (NeurIPS 2020), which combined parametric and non-parametric memory for generation.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; primary; 2020-05-22; usable by 2026-06-14]
   arXiv 2005.11401, v1 submitted 2020-05-22, v4 2021-04-12. Twelve authors led by Patrick Lewis. The fetch tool truncated the abstract; check wording on arXiv before quoting.
   https://arxiv.org/abs/2005.11401
5. **Few-shot prompting originates in Brown et al. 2020, where tasks are specified through demonstrations in the prompt.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; primary; 2020-05-28; usable by 2026-06-14]
   arXiv 2005.14165, submitted 2020-05-28. First authors Tom B. Brown, Benjamin Mann, Nick Ryder.
   Quote: "tasks and few-shot demonstrations specified purely via text interaction with the model"
   https://arxiv.org/abs/2005.14165
6. **The Stanford RegLab hallucination study was published in peer-reviewed form in 2025 in the Journal of Empirical Legal Studies, giving the article a 2025 citation for the 17% to 33% finding.** [CONFIRMED; confidence medium; primary; 2025-06; usable by 2026-06-14]
   Magesh, Surani, Dahl, Suzgun, Manning, Ho; JELS volume 22, issue 2, pages 216 to 242. Not fetched; located through search results including the Wiley DOI page and a Stanford PDF copy at https://dho.stanford.edu/wp-content/uploads/Legal_RAG_Hallucinations.pdf. Confirm the issue month before citing.
   https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/abs/10.1111/jels.12413
7. **OpenAI's Harvey case study is the one documented instance of a legal AI vendor moving beyond retrieval to a custom-trained model, and it was trained on public case law, not a customer's knowledge.** [CORRECTED; confidence high; vendor; 2024-04-02; usable by 2026-06-14]
   Page data gives publicationDateText April 2, 2024. Raw HTML. Harvey first tried fine-tuning and RAG and "ran into limitations"; the custom-trained case law model used "the equivalent of 10 billion tokens worth of data"; Weinberg says lawyers preferred it 97% of the time versus GPT-4. The page says "custom-trained", not "fine-tuned", for the final model.
   Quote: "Recently, Harvey partnered with OpenAI to create a custom-trained case law model."
   https://openai.com/index/harvey/
   FACT-CHECK: Source (April 2, 2024) confirms Harvey and OpenAI built a 'custom-trained case law model' after fine-tuning and RAG 'ran into limitations', trained on Delaware then all U.S. case law with 'the equivalent of 10 billion tokens', preferred '97% of the time'. The source does not say it is the only such instance; that superlative is the researcher's framing and was not verified. Drop or soften 'the one documented instance'. All page details confirmed from raw HTML (publicationDateText April 2, 2024). Only the uniqueness claim is unsupported.
8. **LexisNexis describes Lexis+ AI as retrieval-based, grounding responses in its editorial content through a proprietary RAG platform.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; vendor; 2024-05-03; usable by 2026-06-14]
   Author Serena Wellen, VP Product Management. Read through the fetch tool. Useful as the vendor's own statement of which approach it uses; Thomson Reuters makes a similar RAG statement only on an undated law-school page.
   Quote: "RAG is a technique for enhancing the accuracy and reliability of Gen AI models with facts fetched from authoritative sources."
   https://www.lexisnexis.com/community/insights/legal/b/product-features/posts/how-lexis-ai-delivers-hallucination-free-linked-legal-citations
9. **Microsoft Learn explains chunking as partitioning documents to stay under embedding and chat model token limits, and defines vectors as numeric representations clustered by similarity.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; primary; 2026-06-08; usable by 2026-09-01]
   Azure AI Search chunking page, ms.date 2026-06-08, updated_at 2026-08-31, raw text; recommends starting at 512 tokens with 25% overlap. The semantic index page (ms.date 2026-04-23) supplies the vector definition and says semantic indexing "provides the grounding data for knowledge retrieval via Microsoft Copilot".
   Quote: "Partitioning large documents into smaller chunks can help you stay under the maximum token input limits of chat completion and embedding models."
   https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/search/vector-search-how-to-chunk-documents
10. **OWASP's Top 10 for LLM Applications lists vector and embedding weaknesses, including access-control failures and cross-group leakage in shared vector stores, and prescribes permission-aware stores with strict partitioning.** [CONFIRMED; confidence medium; other; 2024-11; usable by 2026-06-14]
   Non-vendor standards source that replaces the Hintyr vendor blog for the ethical walls point. Page undated; the 2025 list was released in November 2024 (from memory, not verified). Example scenario: embeddings from one group "might be inadvertently retrieved in response to queries from another group's LLM". Read through the fetch tool.
   Quote: "Implement fine-grained access controls and permission-aware vector and embedding stores."
   https://genai.owasp.org/llmrisk/llm08-excessive-agency/
11. **Microsoft's semantic index honors the user identity-based access boundary so grounding only reaches content the user is authorized to see, and indexing does not change permissions.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; primary; 2026-04-23; usable by 2026-09-01]
   Raw text; ms.date 2026-04-23, updated_at 2026-08-18. Also: "Semantic indexing works only with content to which your users already have permission" and "Sensitivity labels are also included in search trimming". Third-party connector content is indexed "while maintaining access controls for content."
   Quote: "we continue to honor the user identity-based access boundary so that the grounding process only accesses content that the current user is authorized to access"
   https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoftsearch/semantic-index-for-copilot
12. **Permission trimming in Microsoft Graph connectors works because every indexed external item carries an access control list of grants and denies, with deny taking precedence.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; primary; 2024-11-07; usable by 2026-06-14]
   Raw text; ms.date 2024-11-07, updated 2025-08-06. Non-directory groups (such as ethical-wall teams) are replicated through external groups. This is the neutral mechanism description the article can cite for how enterprise search enforces permissions.
   Quote: "The access control list (ACL) is used to specify whether the given roles are granted or denied access to view items in Microsoft experiences."
   https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/connecting-external-content-manage-items
13. **Microsoft Copilot operates within existing permissions, and Microsoft warns that overshared or poorly governed content affects results and raises risk.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; primary; 2026-08-17; usable by 2026-09-01]
   Raw text; ms.date 2026-08-17. "Copilot only accesses data that users are authorized to access". Supports the article's point that curation includes fixing oversharing before AI reads the corpus.
   Quote: "Overshared or poorly governed content can affect Copilot results and increase risk."
   https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365/copilot/security-microsoft-365-copilot
14. **A UT Austin preprint (ConfusedPilot) documents a confidentiality vulnerability in RAG systems where retrieval caching leaks data, with Microsoft 365 Copilot as the studied setting.** [CONFIRMED; confidence medium; primary; 2024-08-09; usable by 2026-06-14]
   arXiv 2408.04870, v1 2024-08-09, v5 2024-10-23. Author list (RoyChowdhury, Luo, Sahu, Banerjee, Tiwari) taken from search results; confirm on arXiv. Academic complement to the OWASP entry.
   Quote: "a vulnerability that leaks secret data, which leverages the caching mechanism during retrieval"
   https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.04870
15. **Harbor's ILTACON 2026 brief (Justin Farmer, 2026-09-11) is confirmed verbatim on clean connected data, the recurring deliverable tactic, and fragmented governance ownership across IT, Risk, the GC's office, and knowledge management.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; vendor; 2026-09-11; usable by 2026-09-14]
   Raw HTML. Also confirmed: "Governance needs a clear owner and a defined, visible conversation." and "point it at a broken one and it will still return a confident answer, just a wrong one." New detail: Harbor's own report sentence reads "0% of participating top-tier law firms and global in-house departments have a mature framework for measuring AI's business impact", so Harbor's participants include in-house departments; Embry's quotation says "No participating firm". Quote one exactly.
   Quote: "fragmented ownership of AI security and governance decisions across IT, Risk, the General Counsel's office, and knowledge management"
   https://harborglobal.com/insights/briefs/iltacon-2026-why-ai-usage-isnt-the-same-as-ai-value/
16. **CCBJ's "record quality" sentence is Kristin Calve's own, following Paul Walker's remarks; the 2026-08-31 date and the Walker quotes are confirmed.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; trade-press; 2026-08-31; usable by 2026-09-01]
   Raw HTML. The fetch tool wrongly attributed the sentence to Walker this round; the raw text shows it is unquoted narrative. Walker (iManage): "We're right back at taxonomy," "Now it provides context for AI," "It makes the AI safe to operate," and the corporates remark about communications that "never got into the matter file."
   Quote: "The bottleneck may not always be model quality; it may be record quality."
   https://ccbjournal.com/blog/matter-centricity-2-0-legal-ai-rediscovers-the-matter
17. **CCBJ's "Good Enough" piece (2026-08-31) is confirmed verbatim on what firms cannot swap out: documents, matter histories, work product, institutional knowledge, and access rules.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; trade-press; 2026-08-31; usable by 2026-09-01]
   Raw HTML. Also: "How much better does the result need to be to justify paying more for it?" and "value doesn't disappear with the interface. It settles deeper into the stack."
   Quote: "decades of documents, matter histories, work product and institutional knowledge and a clean answer to who is entitled to see what"
   https://ccbjournal.com/blog/at-iltacon-legal-ai-confronts-good-enough
18. **CCBJ's NetDocuments interview (2026-08-31) confirms Baxter's "rented" models, the Am Law 20 firm with 48 AI applications, and the "micro and incomplete DMSs" phrase for per-application copies of knowledge.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; trade-press; 2026-08-31; usable by 2026-09-01]
   Raw HTML. Baxter: "That intelligence, that context, that's going to have a place of permanence," and "It is the most valuable asset an organization has." Calve on the vendor benchmark: "It is a vendor-run benchmark and should be read as such". The earlier notes' sentence about enterprise data strategy was not re-located; check before quoting.
   Quote: "micro and incomplete DMSs"
   https://ccbjournal.com/blog/rented-models-enduring-memory
19. **The CLOC Global Institute 2026 knowledge session led by JPMorganChase and Guggenheim staff is confirmed from the live agenda feed: Tuesday 2026-05-12, 2:15 to 3:00 PM, room W178, tagged Knowledge Management and Business Intelligence.** [CORRECTED; confidence high; primary; 2026-04-27; usable by 2026-06-14]
   Sessionboard feed behind globalinstitute.cloc.org/full-program (record updated 2026-04-27). Speakers: Jamal Brown (Head of Legal Operations, Guggenheim Investments; his bio in the same record says JPMorgan Chase, so employer is uncertain), Sadie Khodorkovsky (MD eDiscovery Legal Operations and Knowledge Management, JPMorganChase), Leslie Jordan (Knowledge Management Librarian, JPMorganChase), Jen Hugo (VP Legal Operations, JPMorganChase). Track: AI Training, Implementation & Governance. Description includes "Currently, legal operations struggle with fragmented information and missed collaboration opportunities."
   Quote: "This transforms static archives into dynamic, searchable knowledge bases."
   https://globalinstitute.cloc.org/full-program/
   FACT-CHECK: Session SESS-159 'Supercharge Legal Operations: Harness Legal Knowledge Like Never Before', Tuesday May 12, 2026, 02:15 PM to 03:00 PM America/Chicago, location W178, track 'AI Training, Implementation & Governance', record updated 2026-04-27. Tags are 'Knowledge Management' and 'Technology', not Business Intelligence; Business Intelligence appears only in the CLOC Core 12 mapping ('Business Intelligence, Knowledge Management, Strategic Planning, Technology'). Read directly from the Sessionboard embed JSON (api.sessionboard.com/embed/v2/bf699bda-0971-49fd-a6eb-b9e274de521d). Speakers and titles match; Jamal Brown record says company Guggenheim Investments while his bio says JPMorgan Chase, so the employer discrepancy stands. Both quoted description sentences are verbatim.
20. **Stephen Embry's Above the Law piece is dated 2026-08-25 and presents the "mature framework" sentence as a quotation from Harbor, while the 41% and 5% to 6% figures are his own summary sentences.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; trade-press; 2026-08-25; usable by 2026-09-01]
   Raw HTML (page returned 200 to curl this round). Embry: "without obtaining the methodology and information about the data, we have to take them with a grain of salt." He also quotes Harbor: "Firms are measuring AI activity more effectively than AI impact."
   Quote: "No participating firm in Harbor's 2026 legal lab reported having a mature framework for measuring AI's business impact."
   https://abovethelaw.com/2026/08/law-firms-are-buying-ai-but-are-they-really-using-it/
21. **CLOC announced its practitioner-led AI Intensive on 2026-08-19, with a first workshop on 2026-10-27 in New York for about 40 participants covering prompting, workflow and agent design, and implementation planning; the release does not mention knowledge management.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; primary; 2026-08-19; usable by 2026-09-01]
   PRWeb release, dateline San Jose, Aug. 19, 2026. Quotes Oyango Snell (President and CEO) and Alex Gao (board member; Senior Director, Legal Operations, Hilton Worldwide). Mentions an online hub with "governance templates, benchmarking reports" and "Core 12 competency pathways and certification curriculum." Usable in place of the 2026-09-16 certification release.
   Quote: "a program created and led by legal operations practitioners to help teams move beyond AI experimentation into full enterprise deployment"
   https://www.prweb.com/releases/cloc-launches-ai-intensive-practitioner-led-ai-training-for-legal-operations-302854864.html
22. **In the CLOC 2025 State of the Industry Report (186 departments), 79% of legal operations teams count knowledge management among their responsibilities, the sixth most common of 15 services.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; primary; 2025-02-12; usable by 2026-06-14]
   Raw PDF text, page 12 chart "WHICH SERVICES FALL WITHIN YOUR LEGAL OPERATIONS RESPONSIBILITIES?". Order: Outside Counsel/Vendor Management 95%, Technology Strategy 91%, Technology Administration 88%, Program/Project Management 84%, Financial Management 80%, Knowledge Management 79%, Strategic Planning 71%, Training & Development 71%, Business Intelligence 65%. Data from the 2024 Harbor Law Department Survey; released 2025-02-12. This is the survey evidence the idea file listed as missing on legal ops owning knowledge; it does not measure KM headcount or AI curation.
   Quote: "At least 50% of departments have 12 of the 15 services listed below within their legal operations team's responsibilities."
   https://cloc.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/02/2025-CLOC-2025-SOTI-Report.pdf
23. **Knowledge management as a legal operations responsibility rose from 71% to 79% between the 2024 and 2025 CLOC reports, per Stephen Embry's reading.** [UNVERIFIED; confidence medium; trade-press; 2025-05-09; usable by 2026-06-14]
   Trade summary; the 71% prior-year figure was not checked against the 2024 report PDF. Embry also notes business intelligence rose from 58% to 65%.
   Quote: "Knowledge management went from 71% to 79%, suggesting AI tools and data may enable legal ops professionals to play a greater role here"
   https://abovethelaw.com/2025/05/interpreting-the-2025-cloc-in-house-survey-results/
   FACT-CHECK:  Embry's sentence exists (2025-05-09) and reads 'knowledge management went from 71% to &79%' (stray ampersand in source), plus the 58% to 65% BI line. The 71% baseline could not be checked: the 2024 CLOC report PDF is not publicly reachable or archived. Caution: the 2025 report itself says only 'business intelligence and service delivery models have grown by 7% and 5%, respectively, since the previous year' and does not note an 8-point KM rise. Attribute the 71% to Embry only.
24. **The median legal operations function is 5 FTEs (2 FTEs below 3 billion dollars in revenue, up to 10 FTEs above 40 billion), which frames the in-house capacity point.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; primary; 2025-02-12; usable by 2026-06-14]
   Raw PDF text, page 11 "Legal Operations Headcount" of the CLOC 2025 State of the Industry Report. A second table on the same page gives median legal operations professionals by band as 2, 3, 6, and 8 on a different basis; cite the 5-FTE median and the page rather than reconciling.
   Quote: "The size of legal operations teams has remained unchanged since 2023, with a median function size of 5 FTEs."
   https://cloc.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/02/2025-CLOC-2025-SOTI-Report.pdf
25. **The Thomson Reuters 2025 Legal Department Operations Index (128 US respondents, July 2025) reports 82% have at least one dedicated legal ops role and lists knowledge management software among solutions ranked under-utilized relative to value; it gives no KM staffing figure.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; primary; 2025-09; usable by 2026-06-14]
   Raw PDF text. "45% of respondents classify themselves as 'General Counsel tasked to run legal operations'". Confirms the absence of an in-house KM headcount benchmark in this publisher's series.
   Quote: "A large portion (82%) of respondents indicate their legal department has at least one dedicated legal ops role on their team."
   https://www.thomsonreuters.com/en-us/posts/wp-content/uploads/sites/20/2025/09/Legal-Department-Operations-Index-2025.pdf

Gaps:
- A dated, neutral one-sentence definition of grounding that passes the 2026-09-14 display date: Google Cloud's grounding overview is dated 2026-09-18 and Microsoft's Copilot architecture page 2026-09-14; the Microsoft semantic index page (2026-04-23) uses the term without defining it.
- The OpenAI guides (optimizing accuracy, embeddings) and the Anthropic developer docs (context windows, evaluations, API reference) are undated live pages; the dated anchors are Lewis 2020, Brown 2020, and the Anthropic engineering post of 2025-09-29.
- A dated Thomson Reuters statement that CoCounsel or Westlaw AI-Assisted Research is retrieval-based; the law school page is undated and the 2023-11 press release was not read.
- The 71% prior-year knowledge management figure in the CLOC 2024 State of the Industry PDF, and whether the 2026 edition (members-only PDF) carries a knowledge management figure.
- OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications 2025 release date (November 2024 from memory, not verified) and the ConfusedPilot author list (from search results, not the arXiv page).
- No in-house knowledge management headcount or budget benchmark from ACC, CLOC, Thomson Reuters, or Blickstein was found; the ACC and MLA benchmarking release breaks out only litigation and IP lawyers; Blickstein and ACC report pages were blocked or unread.
- Jamal Brown's current employer: the CLOC agenda lists Guggenheim Investments while his bio in the same record says JPMorgan Chase.
- The Vals October 2025 legal research benchmark (VLAIR: Legal Research) was seen only in a LawSites search summary and not fetched.
- The CCBJ sentence about legal's institutional knowledge and enterprise data strategy recorded in the earlier gap track was not re-located in the raw text this round.

Fact-checker overall: 25 findings checked on 2026-09-21: 21 confirmed, 3 corrected, 1 unverified, 0 refuted. Every URL was fetched directly; gated pages (Wiley, OpenAI) were resolved through Crossref, the Stanford PDF, and curl with a browser user agent, and the CLOC agenda was read from the underlying Sessionboard JSON feed. Corrections: (1) Harvey: all page facts hold but 'the one documented instance' is not in the source; (2) CLOC GI session tags are Knowledge Management and Technology, with Business Intelligence only in the Core 12 mapping; (3) the CLOC 2025 report's 2, 3, 6, 8 'second table' does not appear in the PDF text (bands are 2, 4, 7, 10). Unverified: Embry's 71% prior-year KM figure, since the 2024 CLOC report is not publicly available and the 2025 report does not call out KM growth. Freshness: a members-only 2026 CLOC State of the Industry Report (2026-03-02, 135 departments) now exists, so cite the 79% and 5 FTE figures explicitly as the 2025 report. usable_by check: all source dates precede their usable_by dates; every usable_by date is now past, and each source was re-verified today so they can be refreshed to 2026-09-21. Notes written to C:/Users/RALPHS~1/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/c--code-files-spaarke-website/aa6b682a-4844-485c-99e0-cb2228b928c3/scratchpad/research-r2/r2-km-ai-alignment.verified.md. No repository files were modified.


## research:r2-bi-supporting-claims

Part 1: every claim the BI idea file takes from the four researcher tracks was re-fetched. Of 30 rows checked, 27 are confirmed verbatim, 3 are corrected, none refuted. Corrections: (1) H1, the ACC and Everlaw tracking figures (83% spend, 28% time to resolution, 12%, 9%) come from a report dated 2025-11-17 with n=284 (Everlaw release), so they are usable under any display date; the barrier figures (57/50/43/29) appear only in the ACC article of 2026-07-30. (2) T1, Snowflake Intelligence is now rendered "Snowflake CoWork" in the GA release note of 2025-11-04. (3) E24, Elevate's own release frames PayPal as sole winner while both LegalOps.com sources list three winners; follow LegalOps.com. TBD markers cleared by direct inspection: LegalOps.com "Performance Analysis" (seen on the diagram), the RLLB award criteria wording, ACC PDF creation date 2020-09-29, ILTACON dates August 23 to 27 on ILTA's own event page, Compass BI module, the 2020-04-07 CLOC revision, the CLOC session texts (public Sessionboard feed). Part 2: eleven newly usable sources documented with exact figures, including Forrester 2026-08-20 (inside the window, contrary to the brief), Looker Agentic Workflows (preview), Brightflag MCP page (updated 2026-08-18), Gemini Enterprise for Legal (preview, law-firm launch customers), and ILTACON coverage from LawSites, Artificial Lawyer, CCBJ and Above the Law; all ILTACON material is law-firm focused. Part 3: no non-vendor named intake case with numbers dated on or before 2026-09-01 was found; acc.com Value Champion write-ups are blocked; the article should use a labeled composite. Gartner and Gemini glossary remain unreachable.

1. **The ACC and Everlaw measurement figures come from a report published 2025-11-17 with a sample of 284, not from a July 2026 source** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; vendor; 2025-11-17; usable by 2026-06-14]
   Row H1 is corrected. Everlaw's press release (Oakland, November 17, 2025) states the survey basis and confirms 83% track outside counsel spend, 28% time-to-resolution, 12% outside counsel performance, 9% impact on business outcomes, and 96% saying GenAI can help demonstrate value. Report title: The Role of Generative AI in Proving Corporate Law Department Value. The report itself is gated. Everlaw is a vendor co-sponsor; name both ACC and Everlaw.
   Quote: "Based on a survey of 284 CLOs, GCs, and legal operations professionals worldwide"
   https://www.everlaw.com/press/release/gen-ai-accelerates-legal-work-acc-everlaw-survey/

   https://corporatecounselnow.com/your-legal-teams-data-infrastructure-ai-ready

   Bob Ambrogi, August 28, 2026: registrations 5,700, 1,100 more than last year's 4,600; Gaylord Opryland, Nashville. Names the need for better data to drive better AI as a driver of partnerships. Other in-window pieces: Artificial Lawyer day briefings (Aug 25 to 27, written by the Draftwise team, vendor-reported); CCBJ Rented Models, Enduring Memory (Aug 31, Calve: making more of legal's institutional knowledge available as data gives the department another way to participate in enterprise data strategy); Above the Law (Sept 1, 5,782 final attendees). None discusses corporate legal intake, dashboards or BI.
   Quote: "everyone is MCP-ing, API-ing, or otherwise integrating with everyone else."
   https://www.lawnext.com/2026/08/have-we-reached-peak-legal-tech-sure-felt-that-way-at-iltacon-this-week.html
25. **No non-vendor, named corporate legal intake case with numbers dated on or before 2026-09-01 was found; the article should use a labeled illustrative composite** [UNVERIFIED; confidence medium; trade-press; 2026-02-23; usable by none]
   ACC Value Champions 2025 (GlobeNewswire copy of ACC release) and 2026 (via Lexpert, 2026-02-23) name winners without intake figures; Analog Devices' 2026 project covered matter intake and a chatbot but no numbers are in reachable text. Per-champion write-ups on acc.com (including the 2020 Pearson Legal Front Door case) return 403. The only Pearson numbers (35% cost reduction, 30% improved contract turnaround) sit in an Onit and Morae vendor release from 2020. The named ACC cases with numbers (Liberty Mutual, Palo Alto Networks, Mercado Libre) are dated 2026-09-16. Law.com, ABA Journal, Today's General Counsel and CLOC searches returned nothing qualifying.
   Quote: "Analog Devices saved considerable costs, boosted budget predictability, and cut manual effort"
   https://www.lexpert.ca/news/in-house-lawyer/association-of-corporate-counsel-reveals-2026-acc-value-champions/394004
   FACT-CHECK:  Components checked hold: Lexpert (Jacqueline So, 2026-02-23) confirms Analog Devices intake redesign, Checkbox chatbot and the quoted sentence with no numbers; Pearson 35% cost reduction and 30% turnaround are in the Onit and Morae GlobeNewswire release of July 22, 2020; acc.com champion pages return 403. The Liberty Mutual, Palo Alto Networks, Mercado Libre item dated 2026-09-16 could not be located by search (Liberty Mutual was a 2023 Value Champion). A negative finding cannot be proven by fetching, so the overall claim stays unverified though nothing contradicts it.

Gaps:
- Gartner primary text remains unreachable (403): the 2026-05-26 intake forecast release, the 2026-07-28 headcount item, and the glossary definition of BI all rest on trade reprints; the 2026-05-26 date rests on the URL slug only.
- acc.com HTML is blocked to automated tools; the 2026 Value Champions write-ups (Analog Devices intake redesign) and the 2020 Pearson Legal Front Door case may hold intake numbers and need a browser check.
- The ACC and Everlaw report is gated; n=284 and the November 2025 date come from Everlaw's release, and the field dates and geography were not seen.
- The Fabric IQ overview page (ms.date 2026-07-08) was revised on 2026-08-31; whether the quoted sentences existed in the July version cannot be verified without an archive capture.
- The ILTA 2025 executive summary (57% and 54% hurdle figures) was read from a third-party mirror at bmtcorp.com, not from ILTA's own copy.
- No source counts how many legal departments use enterprise BI platforms such as Power BI or Tableau; no source reports board or audit committee reporting drawn from legal data. Present both without citation.
- Legal IT Insider published no ILTACON 2026 recap inside the window; its main piece is dated 2026-09-02.
- The 13% agentic AI figure attributed to the ILTA 2026 survey is not in the executive summary; it appears only in eDiscovery Today (2026-09-14), which is outside the window anyway.

Fact-checker overall: 25 findings checked against their sources on 2026-09-21. 19 confirmed, 5 corrected on details (Ossie draft-status source, RLLB byline and which page carries which criteria wording, Gartner five-forces wording, Gemini Enterprise for Legal component list and the Thomson Reuters connector name), 1 unverified (the negative claim about named intake cases, where one cited 2026-09-16 source could not be located). No finding was refuted. usable_by cautions: the LegalOps.com diagram file is dated 2026-07-04 against a 2026-06-14 usable_by with an undated page; the Power BI overview page's own date (2026-08-24) is after its 2026-07-10 usable_by and both Microsoft Learn pages were revised in late August, so cite them with access dates. Several sources block WebFetch but respond to curl with a browser UA (LegalOps.com, Harbor, CLOC); gartner.com and acc.com champion pages block both. URL corrections: CLOC BI page is /business-intelligence-2/, SMBtech is smbtech.au, CCBJ piece is under /blog/, Power BI standalone page canonicalizes to /explore-reports/. Notes written to C:/Users/RALPHS~1/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/c--code-files-spaarke-website/aa6b682a-4844-485c-99e0-cb2228b928c3/scratchpad/research-r2/r2-bi-supporting-claims.verified.md with downloaded evidence files alongside.


## research:r2-ontology-date-discipline

Part A. The Wayback CDX API was offline, so snapshots were found through the availability API and, more precisely, through the public MicrosoftDocs/fabric-docs repository, which dates every revision. The ontology overview (ms.date 2025-10-06) has a 2026-03-06 capture and a 2026-05-14 revision that was live until 2026-07-21; every quoted sentence (definition, entity types/properties/relationships, "concepts (not just tables)", NL2Ontology, manual refresh) is present verbatim. The Fabric IQ overview has a 2026-06-02 capture (ms.date 2026-05-26) and a 2026-07-09 revision (ms.date 2026-07-08) live on 2026-07-13; the three-layer sentence, the semantic-model generation sentence, and the "Ontology (preview) defines core business entities, relationships, properties, rules, and actions" sentence are all verbatim, which clears that TBD. The agent-integration page fails: it was first published 2026-07-23 (its ms.date 2026-07-09 is an authoring date) and has no archive capture; substitutes are the Fabric IQ overview's "every agent starts with the same understanding" sentence and the "Consume ontology as an MCP server" how-to (ms.date 2026-04-14). Part B. Build 2026 GA and ontology preview status, iManage (2026-05-14), and the Gartner release (2026-05-11, read from gartner.com via a 2026-07-09 capture, which also shows Sallam spoke at the London summit) are confirmed from pre-cutoff captures; LawVu (2026-06-02) and Anthropic (2026-05-12) confirmed live. Part C. The 2025-06-18 and 2025-11-25 specifications, the 2026-05-21 release-candidate post, the 2026-06-18 EMA post, and the 2026-03-16 tool-annotations post replace the 2026-07-28 citation.

1. **The Microsoft Learn ontology overview page carried the quoted definition sentences before 2026-07-13; a Wayback capture of 2026-03-06 and the source revision of 2026-05-14 (live until 2026-07-21) both contain them verbatim.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; primary; 2026-03-06; usable by 2026-06-14]
   Capture https://web.archive.org/web/20260306100720/https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/fabric/iq/ontology/overview has ms.date 2025-10-06 and updated_at 2026-02-17. The GitHub text live on 2026-07-13 is https://raw.githubusercontent.com/MicrosoftDocs/fabric-docs/33b11595fc/docs/iq/ontology/overview.md (commit 2026-05-14). Both contain the definition, the things/facts/connections sentence, the query-surface bullet, the NL2Ontology sentence, the data-binding sentence, the manual-refresh note, and the preview banner.
   Quote: "An ontology is a shared, machine-understandable vocabulary of your business."

21. **An MCP maintainers' post of 2026-03-16 states that tool annotations such as read-only and destructive are hints that clients must treat as untrusted, that a tool with no annotations is assumed potentially destructive, and that clients vary in how strictly they honour the defaults; this is a pre-cutoff primary statement of the responsibilities the protocol leaves to clients and operators.** [CONFIRMED; confidence high; primary; 2026-03-16; usable by 2026-06-14]

The capture list is incomplete: LawVu has a capture at 20260608210141, Claude for Legal at 20260715141014, and Gartner at 20260519122104 (earlier than the cited 20260709). 'LawVu and Anthropic pages none' is wrong. Additional captures seen: ontology overview 20251118230025 and 20260818200509; Fabric IQ overview 20260105134926, 20260429235214, 20260602212517, 20260723213046, 20260824205712; iManage 20260611223242; EMA post 20260619180351; RC post 20260716064405. No capture found for the agent-integration page or for the MCP how-to page before 20260729.

Gaps:
- The Wayback CDX API was offline throughout; the 'latest capture on or before 2026-07-13' figures come from point probes of the availability API and should be re-confirmed with CDX when it is back, in particular that no capture of the ontology overview exists between 2026-03-06 and 2026-07-13 and that the agent-integration page has no capture at all.
- No archive capture exists for the LawVu MCP announcement or the Anthropic Claude for Legal page; both were read live on 2026-09-21 and their dates rest on page metadata and visible date lines, not on a third-party timestamp.
- The MCP blog posts 'MCP joins the Agentic AI Foundation' (2025-12-09) and 'Evolving OAuth Client Registration' (2025-08-22) were listed from the RSS feed but not read; the idea file's background sentence that MCP was introduced by Anthropic in November 2024 still lacks a verbatim primary statement, although the 2025-11-25 anniversary post's title supports the month.
- The 2025-11-25 version of the MCP security best practices page was not read (its source file was removed on 2026-03-25); the 2025-06-18 version was read from the GitHub source at commit d1935d2ce9.
- The Microsoft Fabric blog and Fabric community blog were not attempted in this pass; the Azure blog by Arun Ulag is the only Microsoft primary used for Build 2026. No Microsoft source dated on or before 2026-07-13 announcing ontology GA was found, which is negative evidence only.
- Trade-press commentary that the 2026-07-28 MCP release shifts security responsibility to developers and operators (SecurityWeek via The Legal Wire, 2026-08-03) is after the cutoff; no pre-cutoff third-party commentary making that point was sought in this pass, so the article would make it from the 2025-06-18 audience sentence and the 2026-03-16 annotations post.
